<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CDFD491B-E050-44A6-902D-95DE23E4196F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Calculate the missing angle in this isosceles triangle.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the missing angle $= a$

As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a  + a + 96                             &= 180   \\\\
2a + 96                                 &= 180  \\\\
2a                                      &= 180 - 96  \\\\
2a                                      &= 84  \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {84} {2}  \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {42 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
a                                       &= 42  \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Missing angle $= 42  \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the missing angle $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

2a                                      &= 84  \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {84} {2}  \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac {42 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
a                                       &= 42  \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing angle $= 42  \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E719F44B-5B35-49B3-844A-3A6796E6666A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

What is the value of the missing angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


Let the missing angle $= a$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 90  + 23             &= 180  \\\\
a                        &= 180 - 90 - 23 \\\\
a                        &= 67 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Missing angle $= 67 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$67 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line
<emptyline>
Let the missing angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 90  + 23             &= 180  \\\\
a                        &= 180 - 90 - 23 \\\\
a                        &= 67 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing angle $= 67 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$67 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9FDBD8FE-49B3-4EE9-9569-87D2186CDEEC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Estimate the size of angle $r$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles around a point add up to} = 360 \degree$


The acute angle is approximately $60 \degree$.

Using that we get,

$
\begin{aligned}
r + 60      &= 360 \\\\
r           &= 360 - 60 \\\\
            &= 300 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$300 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_around_a_point
<emptyline>
The acute angle is approximately $60 \degree$.
<emptyline>
Using that we get,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
r + 60      &= 360 \\\\
r           &= 360 - 60 \\\\
            &= 300 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$300 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-86877522-C3FB-43F4-AD0D-8D56F9CB41E2'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Work out the angle marked $?$.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



*TODO:: Add an image with vertices marked ABCD. The smaller trialge is ABD and the bigger one is ABC.*


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram, the triangle $ABD$ is an isosceles triangle.

Let the missing angle $= a$

Let the angle in the isosceles triangle $ABD = b$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin {aligned}
b + b + 62      &= 180 \\\\
2b + 62         &= 180 \\\\
2b              &= 180 - 62 \\\\
2b              &= 118 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {118} {2} \\\\
b               &= 59 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $b$ in the triangle $ABC$.

$
\begin {aligned}
b + 32 + 62 + a     &= 180 \\\\
59 + 32 + 62 + a    &= 180 \\\\
153 + a             &= 180 \\\\
a                   &= 180 - 153 \\\\
a                   &= 27 \degree
\end {aligned}
$


Missing angle $= 27 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_starts
<emptyline>
*TODO:: Add an image with vertices marked ABCD. The smaller trialge is ABD and the bigger one is ABC.*
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_ends
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
Let the missing angle $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the angle in the isosceles triangle $ABD = b$
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
b + b + 62      &= 180 \\\\
2b + 62         &= 180 \\\\
2b              &= 180 - 62 \\\\
2b              &= 118 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {118} {2} \\\\
b               &= 59 \degree
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's use the value of $b$ in the triangle $ABC$.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
b + 32 + 62 + a     &= 180 \\\\
59 + 32 + 62 + a    &= 180 \\\\
153 + a             &= 180 \\\\
a                   &= 180 - 153 \\\\
a                   &= 27 \degree
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>

Missing angle $= 27 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B34288DD-BB7A-4E73-B397-2F8478A7D5D8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Two angles of a triangle are $49 \degree$ and $52 \degree$. What is the third angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
49 + 52 + a    &= 180 \\\\
101 + a        &= 180 \\\\
a              &= 180 - 101 \\\\
a              &= 79 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Third angle $= 79 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$79 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
49 + 52 + a    &= 180 \\\\
101 + a        &= 180 \\\\
a              &= 180 - 101 \\\\
a              &= 79 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Third angle $= 79 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$79 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-D89D0F88-FC46-4178-A22E-462028F52077'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Look at triangle $ABC$.

$ABD$ is an **isosceles** triangle where $AB = AD$.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Work out the sizes of angles $x, y$ and $z$.

Give reasons for your answers.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


$ABD$ is an **isosceles** triangle. So,

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x                                       &= 74 \degree \\\\
x  + y + 74                             &= 180 \\\\
74 + y + 74                             &= 180 \\\\
y + 148                                 &= 180 \\\\
y                                       &= 180 - 148 \\\\
y                                       &= 32 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $x$ and $y$ in triangle ABC.

$
\begin{aligned}
x + y + z + 28                              &= 180 \\\\
74 + 32 + z + 28                            &= 180 \\\\
z + 134                                     &= 180 \\\\
z                                           &= 180 - 134 \\\\
z                                           &= 46 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$x = 74 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$y = 32 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$z = 46 \degree$

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-FEFD6CFA-CB81-4C1B-8D8E-B3859170445C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

One of the angles in an isosceles triangle is $30 \degree$. Which of these is a possible other angle in the triangle:

-  $90 \degree$
-  $80 \degree$
-  $150 \degree$ 
-  $60 \degree$ 
-  $75 \degree$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the other angle $= a$

As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 30      &= 180 \\\\
2a + 30         &= 180 \\\\
2a              &= 180 - 30 \\\\
2a              &= 150 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {150} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {75 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {75 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a               &= 75 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Other angle $= 75 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the other angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 30      &= 180 \\\\
2a + 30         &= 180 \\\\
2a              &= 180 - 30 \\\\
2a              &= 150 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {150} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {75 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {75 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a               &= 75 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Other angle $= 75 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$75 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-53DC209F-8A5F-49BF-8896-B33B61A97645'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Two of the angles in a triangle are $25 \degree$ and $107 \degree$. The third angle is:

-  $28 \degree$ 
-  $48 \degree$ 
-  $68 \degree$ 
-  $88 \degree$ 
-  $180 \degree$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
25 + 107 + a &= 180 \\\\
132 + a      &= 180 \\\\
a            &= 180 - 132 \\\\
a            &= 48 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Third angle $= 48 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
25 + 107 + a &= 180 \\\\
132 + a      &= 180 \\\\
a            &= 180 - 132 \\\\
a            &= 48 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Third angle $= 48 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7D88A99B-2884-41F7-AFBF-593C0094BC10'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A right angled triangle has an angle of $55 \degree$.

What is the size of the smallest angle in the triangle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the smallest angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + 55 + 90     &= 180 \\\\
a + 145         &= 180 \\\\
a               &= 180 - 145 \\\\
a               &= 35 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

Smallest angle $= 35 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the smallest angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + 55 + 90     &= 180 \\\\
a + 145         &= 180 \\\\
a               &= 180 - 145 \\\\
a               &= 35 \degree
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Smallest angle $= 35 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2AC84B68-E32F-46C2-93D6-9D54A566CC18'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A right angled triangle has an angle of $55 \degree$.

What is the size of the smallest angle in the triangle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the smallest angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + 55 + 90     &= 180 \\\\
a + 145         &= 180 \\\\
a               &= 180 - 145 \\\\
a               &= 35 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

Smallest angle $= 35 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the smallest angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + 55 + 90     &= 180 \\\\
a + 145         &= 180 \\\\
a               &= 180 - 145 \\\\
a               &= 35 \degree
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Smallest angle $= 35 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-BB039FA3-8209-4CF6-9799-A0C653637CC6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Calculate the angles labelled $a$ to $f$ in the diagrams below. The diagrams are **NOT DRAWN TO SCALE** :

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As per the first diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 151                 &= 180 \\\\
a                       &= 180 - 151 \\\\
a                       &= 29 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $a$ to find $b$.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 180 \\\\
29 + b      &= 180 \\\\
b           &= 180 - 29 \\\\
b           &= 151 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

As per the second diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
c + 110 + 42    &= 180 \\\\
c + 152         &= 180 \\\\
c               &= 180 - 152 \\\\
c               &= 28 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$c$ and $e$ are alternate angles,

$e = c = 28 \degree$

Similarly, 

$d = 42 \degree$

Now let's find angle $f$,

$
\begin {aligned}
d + e + f   &= 180 \\\\
42 + 28 + f &= 180 \\\\
70 + f      &= 180 \\\\
f           &= 180 - 70 \\\\
f           &= 110 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>a</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$29 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>b</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$151 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>c</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$28 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>d</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$42 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>e</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$28 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>f</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$110 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4DFDE872-CA9D-4835-9FCF-E33BB1673E98'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the size, in degrees, of the angles labelled $a$ to $f$ in the diagrams below, which are NOT DRAWN TO SCALE:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)




</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As per the first diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 62 \degree  &= 180 \\\\
a               &= 180 - 62 \\\\
a               &= 118 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

As per the first diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
b + 43 \degree  &= 180  \\\\
b               &= 180 - 43 \\\\
b               &= 137 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

As per the second diagram, angle $c$ is in isosceles triangle. So,

$c = 71 \degree$

Let's use the value of $c$ to find $d$.

As per the second diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
d + c + 71              &= 180   \\\\
d + 71 + 71             &= 180   \\\\
d + 142                 &= 180   \\\\
d                       &= 180  - 142  \\\\
d                       &= 38 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$c$ and $e$ are vertically opposite angles. So,

$e = c = 71 \degree$

$71 \degree$ and $f$ are corresponding angles. So,

$f = 71 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>a</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$118 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>b</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$137 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>c</p>
</div>

</div>
<div class='option'>

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$38 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>e</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$71 \degree$

</div>
<div class='option'>
<p>f</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$71 \degree$

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-6419C0CA-625C-4E98-89C3-02A997864D0B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the sizes of angles $x$ and $y$.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 104     &= 180   \\\\
x           &= 180  - 104  \\\\
x           &= 76 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $x$ to find $y$.

$
\begin{aligned}
x  + y + y              &= 180 \\\\
76  + 2y                &= 180 \\\\
2y                      &= 180  - 76 \\\\
2y                      &= 104  \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {104 } {2}   \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {52 \times 2} {2}   \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {52 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                       &= 52 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$x$ $=$ $76 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$y$ $=$ $52 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x + 104     &= 180   \\\\
x           &= 180  - 104  \\\\
x           &= 76 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's use the value of $x$ to find $y$.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x  + y + y              &= 180 \\\\
76  + 2y                &= 180 \\\\
2y                      &= 180  - 76 \\\\
2y                      &= 104  \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {104 } {2}   \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {52 \times 2} {2}   \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {52 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                       &= 52 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$y$</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the missing angle $x \degree$. Explain how you did it.

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-40FF7E29-DBCE-405A-9982-1F39CD4E3D5E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the missing angles $a \degree$ and $b \degree$ :

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


-  $a = 48 \degree, \ b = 53 \degree$
-  $a = 48 \degree, \ b = 63 \degree$
-  $a = 58 \degree, \ b = 53 \degree$
-  $a = 58 \degree, \ b = 63 \degree$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 132 &= 180 \\\\
a       &= 180 - 132 \\\\
a       &= 48 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $a$ to find $b$.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + 69                                              &= 180 \\\\
48 + b + 69                                             &= 180 \\\\
b + 117                                                 &= 180 \\\\
b                                                       &= 180 - 117 \\\\
b                                                       &= 63 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$a = 48 \degree, b = 63 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 132 &= 180 \\\\
a       &= 180 - 132 \\\\
a       &= 48 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's use the value of $a$ to find $b$.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + 69                                              &= 180 \\\\
48 + b + 69                                             &= 180 \\\\
b + 117                                                 &= 180 \\\\
b                                                       &= 180 - 117 \\\\
b                                                       &= 63 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$a = 48 \degree, b = 63 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-6F6B6B48-EC9B-41A8-8A65-09482A6B04A0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the missing angles a ̊and b ̊:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


%format% - a = 48o, b = 53o
%format% - a = 48o, b = 63o
%format% - a = 58o, b = 53o
%format% - a = 58o, b = 63o

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 132                                                &= 180 \\\\
a                                                      &= 180 - 32 \\\\
a                                                      &= 48 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $a$ to find $b$.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + 69                                              &= 180 \\\\
48 + b + 69                                             &= 180 \\\\
b + 117                                                 &= 180 \\\\
b                                                       &= 180 - 117 \\\\
b                                                       &= 63 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$a = 48 \degree, b = 63 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>

b                                                       &= 63 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-45D6CC57-CF40-4978-B0E0-366243A0E33D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What is the size of the angle labelled $x$ in the diagram? The diagram is not drawn to scale.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Add an image with angles inside of the quadrilateral marked as "a, b, c" except the one which include x *

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a quadrilateral} = 360 \degree$


Let three angles of quadrilateral $= a,b,c$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 145 &= 180 \\\\
a       &= 180 - 145 \\\\
a       &= 35 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Similarly, we can calculate $b$ and $c$ too.

$b = 35 \degree$

$c = 35 \degree$

Let's use the value of $a$, $b$, and $c$ to find $x$.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + c + 180 + x         &= 360 \\\\
35 + 35 + 35 + 180 + x      &= 360 \\\\
x + 285                     &= 360 \\\\
x                           &= 360 - 285 \\\\
x                           &= 75 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_starts

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-3F849C5D-4F08-4C1D-8B66-7345401C3CFA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Calculate the size of the missing angle in this triangle. The diagram is **not** drawn to scale.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the missing angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 85 + 33 &= 180 \\\\
a + 118     &= 180 \\\\
a           &= 180 - 118 \\\\
a           &= 62 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Missing angle $= 62 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$62 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing angle $= 62 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the size of angle $a$ in the diagram below.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



*TODO:: Add an image with vertices marked ABCD and name the third angle of right angled triangle x.
The right angled trianlge is ABD and the bigger one is ABC.*


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the third angle in triangle $ABD = a$

As per the diagram, in triangle $ABD$,

$
\begin {aligned}
b + 90 + 19         &= 180 \\\\
b + 109             &= 180 \\\\
b                   &= 180 - 109 \\\\
b                   &= 71 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $b$ in triangle $ABC$.

As per the diagram, in triangle $ABC$,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b + 17 + 72             &= 180 \\\\
a + 71 + 17 + 72            &= 180 \\\\
a + 160                     &= 180 \\\\
a                           &= 180 - 160 \\\\
a                           &= 20 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \degree$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Would you describe $100\degree$? Acute, obtuse or reflex?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Angle greater than $90 \degree$ and less than $180 \degree$ is called obtuse angle.

Therefore, $100\degree$ is an obtuse angle.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Obtuse angle

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C2C2B5E4-3444-4E62-A460-C54A7D2EB3FB'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

How many right angles are there in two complete turns?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin {aligned}
\text{One complete turn}                    &= 360 \degree \\\\
\text{One right angle}                      &=90 \degree \\\\
\text {Right angles in two turns}           &= \dfrac {360 \times 2} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 2} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times 4 \times 2} {\cancel {90}} \\\\
                                            &= 4 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{One complete turn}                    &= 360 \degree \\\\
\text{One right angle}                      &=90 \degree \\\\
\text {Right angles in two turns}           &= \dfrac {360 \times 2} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 2} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times 4 \times 2} {\cancel {90}} \\\\
                                            &= 4 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DA636143-50D9-44F2-92E7-6E0251062FF1'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


ABCDEF is a regular hexagon. What is the size of angle $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Exterior Angle} = \dfrac{360 \degree} {\text{Number of sides}}$



$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {60 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {60 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {60 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
            &= 60 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_polygon_exterior_angle
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {60 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {60 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {60 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
            &= 60 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-72ECFDB7-86F7-48D5-9A2F-0BB4E6085F9C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If two angles of a triangle add up to $137 \degree$, what is the size of the third angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the size of the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 137      &= 180  \\\\
a            &= 180 - 137  \\\\
a            &= 43 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Third angle $= 43 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$43 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the size of the third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 137      &= 180  \\\\
a            &= 180 - 137  \\\\
a            &= 43 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Third angle $= 43 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$43 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-64FD1EC6-ED8D-44D6-BD41-D17B07CD9FE4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


ABCDEF is a regular hexagon. What is the size of angle $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Interior Angle} = \dfrac{\text{(Sides - 2)} \times 180 \degree} {\text{Sides}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= \dfrac {(6 - 2) \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
            &= 4 \times 30 \\\\
            &= 120 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_polygon_interior_angle
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= \dfrac {(6 - 2) \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
            &= 4 \times 30 \\\\
            &= 120 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DBB54740-C177-4685-AD73-ECFA3880007B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If two angles of a triangle add up to $49 \degree$, what is the size of the third angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the size of the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 49       &= 180  \\\\
a            &= 180 - 49  \\\\
a            &= 131 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Third angle $= 131 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$131 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the size of the third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 49       &= 180  \\\\
a            &= 180 - 49  \\\\
a            &= 131 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Third angle $= 131 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$131 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-011C7343-EA2D-4EDF-AFE0-7803C6D34E4D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If two angles of a triangle are $36 \degree$ and $22 \degree$, what is the size of the third angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the size of the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 36 + 22     &= 180  \\\\
a + 58          &= 180  \\\\
a               &= 180 - 58  \\\\
a               &= 122 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Third angle $= 122 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$122 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the size of the third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 36 + 22     &= 180  \\\\
a + 58          &= 180  \\\\
a               &= 180 - 58  \\\\
a               &= 122 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Third angle $= 122 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$122 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2AEEFFF0-25FA-4C42-9D96-9B86D7D7FB76'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


ABCDEF is a regular hexagon. What is the size of angle $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Interior Angle} = \dfrac{\text{(Sides - 2)} \times 180 \degree} {\text{Sides}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= \dfrac {(6 - 2) \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
            &= 4 \times 30 \\\\
            &= 120 \degree
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_polygon_interior_angle
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= \dfrac {(6 - 2) \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 180} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times 6} {6} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {4 \times 30 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
            &= 4 \times 30 \\\\
            &= 120 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4B420308-03FA-4469-A36A-B00E5A3340D8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If two angles of a triangle add up to $89 \degree$, what is the size of the third angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the size of the third angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 89       &= 180  \\\\
a            &= 180 - 89  \\\\
a            &= 91 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Third angle $= 91 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$91 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
Let the size of the third angle $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 89       &= 180  \\\\
a            &= 180 - 89  \\\\
a            &= 91 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Third angle $= 91 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$91 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Write down the value of $y$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


Let the third angle of the triangle $= a$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 70 &= 180 \\\\
a      &= 180 - 70 \\\\
a      &= 110 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $a$ to find $y$.

$
\begin{aligned}
y + y + a   &= 180 \\\\
2y + 110    &= 180 \\\\
2y          &= 180 - 110 \\\\
2y          &= 70 \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {70} {2} \\\\
y           &= 35 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 \degree$

</div>
</div>

x           &= 180 - 70 \\\\

$
<emptyline>
Let's use the value of $a$ to find $x$.
<emptyline>
As per the diagram, in triangle $ABD$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
x + a + a   &= 180 \\\\
x + 35 + 35 &= 180 \\\\
x + 70      &= 180 \\\\
x           &= 180 - 70 \\\\
x           &= 110 \degree
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 108 &= 180 \\\\
x       &= 180 - 108 \\\\
x       &= 72 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

x       &= 180 - 108 \\\\

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $y$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
y + y + 108 &= 180 \\\\
2y + 108    &= 180 \\\\
2y          &= 180 - 108 \\\\
2y          &= 72 \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {2 \times 36} {2} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 36} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y           &= 36 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \degree$

</div>
</div>

y           &= 36 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


- $36$
- $64$
- $100$
- $144$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles around a point add up to} = 360 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + (180 - 100) + (360 - 324)              &= 180 \\\\
x + 80 + 36                                &= 180 \\\\
x                                          &= 180 - 80 - 36 \\\\
x                                          &= 64 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$64$

</div>
</div>
